~~~\}

\mathsf{true}~~.

Note that the usage of (
27
) relies on the fact that both
\psi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
and
\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi
are pers.
The straightforward proof is omitted.

\Box

Lemma 44

A relation
R
is isomorphic to a coreflexive iff
R
is a bijection.

Proof
The proof is by mutual implication. Suppose first that
R
is a bijection. That is,

R\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 8.99963pt{\wedge}\hskip 8.99963ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

We prove that
R
is isomorphic to
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
. (Symmetrically,
R
is isomorphic to
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
.) For the witnesses we take

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and
R
. Instantiating definition
38
, we have to verify that

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 7.4997pt{\wedge}\hskip 7.4997pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 7.4997pt{\wedge}\hskip 7.4997ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 7.4997pt{\wedge}\hskip 7.4997ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

and

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

The verification is a straightforward application of properties of the left domain operator.

Now suppose that coreflexive
p
is isomorphic to
R
. Suppose the witnesses are
\phi
and
\psi
. That is,

(45)

\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptp\hskip 7.4997pt{\wedge}\hskip 7.4997pt\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 7.4997pt{\wedge}\hskip 7.4997pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

and

(46)

p\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

Then

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
~~~\}

\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi

=

\{

\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptp\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptp\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptp^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
~~~\}

\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptp\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptp^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi

=

\{

(
46
)
~~~\}

\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi

=

\{

converse
~~~\}

\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi

=

\{

(
45
)
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

domains: (
14
)
~~~\}

R\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

We conclude that
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
. Symmetrically,
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR
. That is,
R
is a bijection.

\Box

Theorem 47

Suppose
P
is a per. Then,

P{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptP\hskip 8.99963pt{\Leftarrow}\hskip 8.99963ptP{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptP~~.

In particular, for all
R
,

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 8.99963pt{\Leftarrow}\hskip 8.99963ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~.

Symmetrically, for all
R
,

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 8.99963pt{\Leftarrow}\hskip 8.99963ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}~~.

Proof
This is an instance of lemma
44
. Specifically, assuming that
P{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptP
, we may
apply the instantiation
p{,}R\hskip 2.99988pt{:=}\hskip 2.99988ptP{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{,}\hskip 1.49994ptP
in lemma
44
to deduce that
P
is a bijection. That
is,
P\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptP^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptP{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
. But
P
is a per (i.e.
P\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptP\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptP^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
). So we conclude that

P\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptP{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~.

That, for all
R
,
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
if
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
now follows by making the instantiation
P\hskip 1.49994pt{:=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and using the fact
that
(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
. The symmetric property of the right domain operators follows by making the
instantiation
P\hskip 1.49994pt{:=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
and using the fact that
(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
.

\Box

4
Indexes and Core Relations

This section introduces the notions of “index” and “core” of a relation. An “index” is a special case of a
“core” of a relation but, in general, it is more useful. The properties of both notions are explored in depth.

4.1
Indexes

Recall fig.
1
. We said that the middle and rightmost figures depict “core relations”.
The property that is common to both is captured by the following definition.

Definition 48
(Core Relation)

A relation
R
is a
core relation
iff
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
.

\Box

The rightmost figure of fig.
1
is what we call an “index” of the relation depicted by the leftmost
figure. The definition of an “index” of a relation is as follows.

Definition 49
(Index)

An
index
of a relation
R
is a relation
J
that has the following properties:

(a)

J\hskip 1.49994pt{\subseteq}\hskip 1.49994ptR~~,

(b)

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptR~~,

(c)

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~,

(d)

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

\Box

Note particularly requirement
49
(a). A consequence of this requirement is that an index of a
relation has the same type as the relation. This means that the relation depicted by the middle figure of
fig.
1
is
not
an index of the relation depicted by the leftmost figure because the relations have
different types.

An obvious property is that a core relation is an index of itself:

Theorem 50

Suppose
R
is a core relation. Then
R
is an index of
R
.

Proof
Straightforward application of definitions
48
and
49
together with the properties
of (coreflexive and per) domains.

\Box

In general, the existence of an index of an arbitrary relation is
not
derivable in systems that axiomatise
point-free relation algebra. In section
5.2
we add a limited form of the axiom of choice
that guarantees the existence of indexes of arbitrary pers; we also show that this then guarantees the
existence of indexes for arbitrary relations. For the moment, we establish a number of properties of
indexes assuming they exist. For example, we show that all indexes of a given relation are isomorphic:
see theorem
60
.

Lemma 51

If
J
is an index of the relation
R
then

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 8.99963pt{\wedge}\hskip 8.99963ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}~~.

It follows that

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 8.99963pt{\wedge}\hskip 8.99963ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}~~.

That is, an index is a core relation.

Proof
We first prove that
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
.

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

=

\{

definition
~~~\}

R/{\hskip-3.41432pt}/{}R\hskip 2.99988pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\supseteq

\{

49
(a) and monotonicity
~~~\}

R/{\hskip-3.41432pt}/{}R\hskip 2.99988pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\supseteq

\{

see below
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~.

The last step in the above calculation proceeds as follows.

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\subseteq}\hskip 5.99976ptR/{\hskip-3.41432pt}/{}R\hskip 2.99988pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\Leftarrow

\{

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
(so
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
) and
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

monotonicity
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\subseteq}\hskip 5.99976ptR/{\hskip-3.41432pt}/{}R

=

\{

definition of
R/{\hskip-3.41432pt}/{}R
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\subseteq}\hskip 5.99976ptR{/}R\hskip 2.99988pt{\cap}\hskip 2.99988pt(R{/}R)^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

=

\{

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\subseteq}\hskip 5.99976ptR{/}R

=

\{

shunting
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 7.4997pt{\subseteq}\hskip 7.4997ptR~~.

We continue with the lefthand side of the above inclusion.

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR

=

\{

49
(b)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and domains: (
14
)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

49
(c)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

(corefexive and per) domains: (
14
) and (
29
)
~~~\}

J\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

\subseteq

\{

49
(a)
~~~\}

R\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

per domains: (
29
)
~~~\}

R~~.

We conclude that
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
. The equation
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
uses anti-symmetry.

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

\supseteq

\{

per domains: (
25
), and reflexivity of
J/{\hskip-3.41432pt}/{}J
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

49
(c)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\supseteq

\{

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
(see above), composition of coreflexives is idempotent
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~.

The other two properties are symmetrical.

\Box

An immediate corollary of lemma
51
is the following theorem.

Theorem 52

If
J
is an index (of some relation) then
J
is an index of
J
.

Proof
Suppose
J
is an index of
R
. Then we have to prove the properties
49
(a), (b), (c) and (d)
with
R\hskip 1.49994pt{:=}\hskip 1.49994ptJ.
These are the properties:

(e)

J\hskip 1.49994pt{\subseteq}\hskip 1.49994ptJ~~,

(f)

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptJ~~,

(g)

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~,

(h)

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

Properties (e) and (f) are true of all relations
J
. Properties (g) and (h) follow from lemma
51

and the fact that composition of coreflexives is idempotent.

\Box

The indexes of a relation are uniquely defined by their left and right domains. See corollary

54
, which is an immediate consequence of the following lemma.

Lemma 53

Suppose
J
is an index of the relation
R
. Then

J\hskip 5.99976pt{=}\hskip 5.99976ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

Proof

J

=

\{

domains: (
14
)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

49
(c) and (d)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

domains: (
14
)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

49
(b)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

\Box

Corollary 54

Suppose
J
and
K
are both indexes of the relation
R
. Then

J\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptK\hskip 11.99951pt{\equiv}\hskip 11.99951ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\wedge}\hskip 5.99976ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

Proof
Implication is an immediate consequence of Leibniz’s rule. For the “if” part, we assume that

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
. Then

J

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
, lemma
53
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

assumption:
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\wedge}\hskip 5.99976ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
~~~\}

K{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

K
is an index of
R
, lemma
53
with
J\hskip 1.49994pt{:=}\hskip 1.49994ptK
~~~\}

K~~.

\Box

The following lemma becomes relevant when we study indexes of difunctions. (See section

5.1
.)

Lemma 56

Suppose
J
is an index of
R
. Then

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
are pers.

Proof
We prove that

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 7.4997pt{=}\hskip 7.4997ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

We have:

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

=

\{

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
is a per,
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
is a coreflexive, converse
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

=

\{

49
(c)
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~.

~~~\}

, domains

~~~\}

=

\{

49
(b)
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~.

\Box

Lemma 58

Suppose

. Then

(b)

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}~~.

Proof

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

=

\{

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
is a per
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

~~~\}

\{

J
is an index of

; definition

~~~\}

\{

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
is a per
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

:

~~~\}

lemma

~~~\}

~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~.

By anti-symmetry of the subset relation we have proved (a). Property (b) is symmetrical.

\Box

Theorem 59

Suppose
J
is an index of
R
. Then
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
is an index of
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
is an index of
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
.

Proof
We prove that
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
is an index of
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
. That
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
is an index of
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
is symmetrical.

Instantiating definition
49
with
R{,}J\hskip 2.99988pt{:=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{,}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
, our task is to prove the four properties:

(a)

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~,

(b)

(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~,

(c)

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~,

(d)

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

The proof of property (a) is straightforward:

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

\Leftarrow

\{

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\subseteq}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
, transitivity
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\subseteq}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\Leftarrow

\{

monotonicity
~~~\}

J\hskip 1.49994pt{\subseteq}\hskip 1.49994ptR

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
,
49
(a)
~~~\}

\mathsf{true}~~.

Property (b) simplifies using the fact that
(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
,
(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
and
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
to:

(b’)

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~,

This is the first of the two properties proved in lemma
58
.
Using the fact that
(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
, property (c) is the same as property (c) of definition

49
; similarly, using the fact that

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
, and
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
, property (d) is also the same as property (c) of definition

49
.

\Box

We show later that the converse of theorem
59
is a prescription for constructing an index of an
arbitrary relation. See theorem
76
.

Theorem 60

If
R
and
S
are isomorphic relations then indexes of
R
and
S
are also
isomorphic. In particular, indexes of a relation
R
are isomorphic.

Proof
Suppose
\phi
and
\psi
witness the isomorphism
R\hskip 1.49994pt{\cong}\hskip 1.49994ptS
and
J
is an index of
R
and
K
is an
index of
S
. We verify that
\lambda
and
\rho
defined by

\lambda\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 10.49957pt{\wedge}\hskip 10.49957pt\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

witness the isomorphism
J\hskip 1.49994pt{\cong}\hskip 1.49994ptK
.

The task is to verify that

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 10.49957pt{\wedge}\hskip 10.49957pt\lambda^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 10.49957pt{\wedge}\hskip 10.49957pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 10.49957pt{\wedge}\hskip 10.49957pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

and

J\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982pt\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptK\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

The four domain properties are all essentially the same so we only verify the first conjunct:

\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

=

\{

definition, converse
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptK{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

K
is an index of
S
, lemma
56
with
J{,}R\hskip 1.49994pt{:=}\hskip 1.49994ptK{,}S
~~~\}

\{

theorem
60
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
is a per,

is an index of

, definition

(c)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~.

\{

definition, converse
~~~\}

=

\{

domains: (
14
)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptK\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

is an index of

, definition

(b)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\phi\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptS\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\psi^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

\{

~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

per domains: (
29
)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
, definition
49
(b)
~~~\}

J~~.

That the indexes of a relation
R
are isomorphic follows because
R
is isomorphic to itself (with witnesses
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

and
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
), i.e. the isomorphism relation is reflexive.

\Box

The construction of the witnesses
\lambda
and
\rho
looks very much like the proverbial rabbit out of a hat! In fact,
they were calculated using the type judgements formulated in Voermans’ thesis
[
Voe99
]
. We hope at a
later date to exploit Voermans’ calculus in order to make the process of constructing witnesses much
more methodical.

4.2
Core Relations

Indexes are a special case of what we call “core” relations. (Recall definition
48
.) This section is
about the properties of a “core” of a given relation
R
, first introduced in
[
BO23
]
.

Definition 61
(Core)

Suppose
R
is an arbitrary relation and suppose
C
is a relation
such that

C\hskip 5.99976pt{=}\hskip 5.99976pt\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

for some relations
\lambda
and
\rho
satisfying

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982pt\lambda^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda\hskip 10.49957pt{\wedge}\hskip 10.49957pt\lambda{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 10.49957pt{\wedge}\hskip 10.49957ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho\hskip 10.49957pt{\wedge}\hskip 10.49957pt\rho{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

Then
C
is said to be a
core of

R

as witnessed by

\lambda

and

\rho
.

\Box

(The terminology just introduced anticipates theorem
65
which establishes
that a core of a relation is indeed a core relation according to definition
48
.)

The existence of a core of a given relation
R
has a constructive element: it is necessary to construct the
“witnesses”
\lambda
and
\rho
. In general, given a per
P
, a functional relation
f
with the property that
P
equals

f^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptf
is called a “splitting” of
P
. Constructing a core of relation
R
thus involves “splitting ” the pers
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

and
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
into functional relations
\lambda
and
\rho
. As with indexes, the existence of cores is not derivable in
point-free relation algebra. However, just as for
indexes, all cores of a given relation are isomorphic in the sense of definition
38
.
See section
6
for further discussion of the construction of cores of pers.

Immediately obvious is that an index of a relation is a core of the relation:

Theorem 62

Suppose
R
is an arbitrary relation and suppose
J
is an index of
R
. Then
J

is a core of
R
as witnessed by
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
.

Proof
First,

J

=

\{

lemma
53
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

per domains: (
29
)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

converse, domains are coreflexive
~~~\}

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

This establishes the required property of
C
in definition
61
, with
C\hskip 1.49994pt{:=}\hskip 1.49994ptJ
.
(The parentheses in the last line of the calculation indicate the definitions of the splittings
\lambda
and
\rho
.)
Second,

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}

=

\{

converse,
(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and
(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

lemma
58
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}~~.

Third,

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

=

\{

converse,
(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
, definition
49
(c)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

theorem
59
; in particular,
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\subseteq}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
~~~\}

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
, domains: theorem
19
(c)
~~~\}

(J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~.

This establishes the required properties of
\lambda
in definition
61
(with
\lambda\hskip 2.99988pt{:=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
).
The properties of
\rho
in definition
61
(with
\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{:=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
) are established similarly.

\Box

Fig.
2
illustrates theorem
62
applied to the relation introduced in
fig.
1
. The index
J
is depicted by the green edges in the lower bipartite graph. The decomposition
of the relation in the definition of a core is illustrated by the row of bipartite graphs at the top;
the relations depicted are, in order,
\lambda^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
,
\lambda
,
R
,
\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
and
\rho
. The composition of the middle three figures is the
index
J
.

Figure 2:
Decomposition of a Relation into a Core and Witnesses

A number of properties of indexes are derived from the fact that indexes are cores. The remainder of
this section catalogues such properties.

The name “core” in definition
61
anticipates theorem
65
where we show
that the relation
C
is a core relation as defined by definition
48
. Some preliminary lemmas are
needed first.

For later use, we calculate the left and right domains of the core of a relation.

Suppose

,

,

domains
~~~\}

\rho{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

=

\{

definition

~~~\}

Lemma 64

Suppose

,
\lambda
,

and

. Suppose also that

. Then

\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
.

Proof

C

=

\{

definition
61
~~~\}

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
, definition
49
(b)
~~~\}

\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

=

\{

definition
61
~~~\}

\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

and

are functional,

and
\rho{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
~~~\}

\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}

=

\{

domains: (
14
) and converse
~~~\}

\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}~~.

Comparing the last line with the definition of an isomorphism of relations (definition
38
with the
instantiation
R{,}S{,}\phi{,}\psi\hskip 4.49982pt{:=}\hskip 4.49982ptC\hskip 2.99988pt{,}\hskip 2.99988ptJ\hskip 2.99988pt{,}\hskip 2.99988pt\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{,}\hskip 2.99988pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
), we postulate

\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and
\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
as witnesses to the isomorphism.

It remains to show that
\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and
\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
are bijections on the appropriate domains. First,

(\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

converse
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

definition
61
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
, definition
49
(d)
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

Symmetrically,

(\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{=}\hskip 5.99976ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~.

Finally,
)

(\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

\rho
is functional, and
\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
,

i.e.
\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
~~~\}

(\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

and
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
~~~\}

(\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

domains: theorem
19
(b) and (c),
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}})^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}
~~~\}

(\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

domains: theorem
19
(c)
~~~\}

(\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt(R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

lemmas
56
and
57
(b)
~~~\}

(\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

(
31
) and domains: theorem
19
(c)
~~~\}

(\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 2.99988ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

\rho\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988pt\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
(see first step)
~~~\}

\rho{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

=

\{

lemma
63
~~~\}

C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}~~.

Symmetrically,
(\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}){\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
.

Putting all the calculations together, we conclude that
\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and
\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
are bijections; the left domain of

\lambda\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
is
C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
and its right domain is
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}
; the left domain of
\rho\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
is
C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
and its right domain is
J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}
.

\Box

We now prove the theorem alluded to by the nomenclature of definition
61
, namely any core of a
given relation
R
is a core relation in the sense of definition
48
.

Theorem 65

Suppose
C
is a core of
R
. Then, if
R
has an index,

(66)

C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\mbox{~~, and}

(67)

C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}~~.

That is, if
R
has an index, any core
C
of
R
is a core relation. (See definition
48
.)

Proof
Assume that
J
is an index of
R
. The proof is a combination of several preceding lemmas and
theorems.

C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{=}\hskip 4.49982ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\Leftarrow

\{

theorem
47
~~~\}

C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 4.49982pt{\cong}\hskip 4.49982ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\Leftarrow

\{

Leibniz
~~~\}

J{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{=}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\wedge}\hskip 5.99976ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\wedge}\hskip 5.99976ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\Leftarrow

\{

index
J
is a core relation (lemma
51
)
~~~\}

C{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 5.99976pt{\wedge}\hskip 5.99976ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptC{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}

\Leftarrow

\{

lemmas
43
and
42
~~~\}

C\hskip 2.99988pt{\cong}\hskip 2.99988ptJ

=

\{

lemma
64
~~~\}

\mathsf{true}~~.

\Box

Note
Theorem
65
assumes that relation
R
has an index
J
. Likewise, a corollary of
lemma
64
is that, assuming relation
R
has an index, all cores of
R
are isomorphic. It is
straightforward to prove that all cores of
R
are isomorphic without the assumption that
R
has
an index. Similarly, theorem
65
can be proved without this assumption but the proof is
quite long and complex. See
[
Bac21
]
for full details.

We argue later that this assumption has no practical significance: in section
5.3
we
show that every relation
R
has an index if both its per domains have an index. This means that, for a
given
R
, it is necessary to calculate indices of
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}
and
R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}
; however, in practice, this is not an issue.

End of Note

5
Indexes of Difunctions and Pers

5.1
Indexes of Difunctions

We now specialise the notion of index to difunctions.

Lemma 68

Suppose
J
is an index of relation
R
and
J
is difunctional. Then
R

is difunctional.

Proof

R\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
, lemma
55
~~~\}

R\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
,
49
(b)
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

domains: (
14
) and theorem
19
(b)
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

J
is an index of
R
,
49
(d) and (c)
~~~\}

R{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\prec}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{>}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{<}$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptR{\mbox{\raisebox{1.70717pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\succ}$}}}

=

\{

domains: (

) and theorem

(b), and
J
is difunctional (i.e.
J\hskip 2.99988pt{=}\hskip 2.99988ptJ\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ^{\mbox{\raisebox{1.42262pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cup$}}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\mbox{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}}\hskip 1.49994ptJ
)
~~~\}
